<debateSection name="#opening">

<heading>FRIDAY, 2nd MARCH, 1945</heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Mr. SPEAKER took the Chair at <recordedTime time="1945-02-02T11:05:00">11.5 a.m.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<p>S.C ON KAMANASSIE IRRIGATION</p>

<debateSection name="#district_adjustment_bill">

<heading>DISTRICT ADJUSTMENT BILL</heading>

<p>Mr. JACKSON, as Chairman, brought up the Report of the Select Committee on the Kamanassie Irrigation District Adjustment Bill, reporting the Bill with amendments.</p>

<p>Report, proceedings and evidence to be printed.</p>

<p>House to go into Committee on the Bill on 15th March.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#questions">

<heading>QUESTIONS</heading>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Companies and Properties Belonging to Indians</heading>

<question by="#nel" to="#minister_of_economic_development">

<from>Mr. NEL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of the Interior:</p>

<ol>

<li>I.

<ol>

<li>(1) What number of companies of which Indians have control are registered in each province; and</li>

<li>(2) what is the value of the share capital of such companies.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>II.

<ol>

<li>(a) How many properties belong to Indians and</li>

<li>(b) what is the estimated value of such properties, in each province.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>III.

<ol>

<li>(a) How many trading stands are held by Indians and</li>

<li>(b) what is the estimated value of such stands, in each province.</li>

</ol>

</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_economic_development" to="#nel">

<from>The MINISTER OF ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<p>I regret to inform the hon. member that the information sought in Questions I, II and III is not available.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Trading Licences Issued to Asiatics</heading>

<question by="#nel" to="#minister_of_finance">

<num>IV.</num>

<from>Mr. NEL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of the Interior :</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many wholesale and retail businesses, respectively, are conducted by Indians in each province; and</li>

<li>(2) how many trading licences were issued to Indians in each province during 1944.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#nel">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) For purposes of the Licences Consolidation Act No. 32 of 1925 (as amended) there is no differentiation between wholesale and retail trading licences, and statistics for a reply to this question are therefore not available.</li>

<li>(2) The numbers of trading licences issued to Asiatics in the various provinces are:&#x2014;</li>

</ol>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Cape Province</p></td>

<td><p>3,293</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Natal</p></td>

<td><p>7,639</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Transvaal</p></td>

<td><p>6,352</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Orange Free State</p></td>

<td><p>Nil.</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The above figures relate to the financial year ended 31st March, 1944.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>VALUE OF SOUTH AFRICAN POUND</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_finance">

<num>VI.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Finance:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether since the beginning of the war to date there has been any change in the value of the South African pound; and, if so,</li>

<li><span class="col_2615-2616" refersTo="page_1317"/>(2) what was the value of the South African pound at the outbreak of war and what is its value at present, as against (a) the American dollar and (b) the British and (c) the Australian pound.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>&#x201C;The Building of a Nation&#x201D; Film</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_transport">

<num>VII.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Transport:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Where was the film &#x201C;The Building of a Nation&#x201D; last shown;</li>

<li>(2) whether the film will again be shown; if so, when;</li>

<li>(3) whether, for educational purposes, he will immediately make arrangements to have it shown again; and, if not,</li>

<li>(4) whether he will hand the film to an Afrikaans film company for showing.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Pretoria West.</li>

<li>(2) to (4) I would refer the hon. member to the reply given to Question No. IX asked on 31st March, 1944.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Cape Town University : Discrimination Re Sports Facilities</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_education">

<num>X.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What was the amount of the grant to the University of Cape Town for the financial year ended 31st March, 1944;</li>

<li>(2) what is the amount of the grant paid or to be paid for the financial year ending 31st March, 1945;</li>

<li>(3) whether the funds are used for the general maintenance of the university including sports facilities;</li>

<li>(4) whether it has been brought to his notice that discrimination is made between European students in respect of the use of certain buildings and grounds; if so,</li>

<li>(5) whether he will make representations for changing the arrangements in regard to the buildings and grounds;</li>

<li>(6) whether he will consider suspending payment of the grant while such arrangements remain unchanged; and, if not, (7) whether he will take any other steps; if so, what steps; if not, why not.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) &#x00A3;100,000.</li>

<li>(2) &#x00A3;100,000.</li>

<li>(3) The University is a statutory institution which applies its monies, including the Government subsidy, in its own discretion.</li>

<li>(4) No, I have no official knowledge of this matter.</li>

<li>(5) Falls away.</li>

<li>(6) and (7) See reply under 3 I do not feel myself called upon as Minister of Education to interefere with the domestic matters of independent statutory bodies.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Use of U.D.F. Broadcasting Unit</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XI.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Posts and Telegraphs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the broadcasting unit used at the Liberty Cavalcade held recently has at any time been used by any other person or body than the U.D.F. Corps of Signals; if so, (a) for what purpose and (b) by whom was it so used; and</li>

<li>(2) whether all its broadcasts were made under authority of the U.D.F. Corps of Signals; if not, which broadcasts were not.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading><span class="col_2617-2618" refersTo="page_1318"/>Taxation on Motor Spirit</heading>

<question by="#shearer" to="#minister_of_finance">

<num>XII.</num>

<from>Dr. V. L. SHEARER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Finance:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) What were the rates of taxation in pence per gallon on motor spirit paid for each of the financial years from 1936 to 1944, inclusive, as (i) customs and (ii) excise duties and (b) at what dates were the rates altered;</li>

<li>(2) (a) what were the rates in pence per gallon on motor spirit paid into the National Roads Fund for the financial years from 1936 to 1944, inclusive of (i) customs and (ii) excise duties and (b) at what dates, if any, were the rates altered;</li>

<li>(3) what were the amounts paid on motor spirit for each of the financial years from 1936 to 1944, inclusive, in (a) customs duties and (b) excise duties; and</li>

<li>(4) what were the amounts of (a) customs and (b) excise duties on motor spirit paid into the National Roads Fund for each of the financial years from 1936 to 1944, inclusive?</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#shearer">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<p>(1) (a):</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(i) Customs per gallon.</p></td>

<td><p>(ii) Excise per gallon.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1936-&#x2019;37</p></td>

<td><p>6d.</p></td>

<td><p>Nil</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1937-&#x2019;38</p></td>

<td><p>6d., 5d.</p></td>

<td><p>Nil</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1938-&#x2019;39</p></td>

<td><p>5d.</p></td>

<td><p>Nil</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1939-&#x2019;40</p></td>

<td><p>5d.</p></td>

<td><p>Nil</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1940-&#x2019;41</p></td>

<td><p>5d., 8d., 9d.</p></td>

<td><p>3d., 4d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1941-&#x2019;42</p></td>

<td><p>9d., 11&#x00BD;d.</p></td>

<td><p>4d., 6&#x00BD;d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1942-&#x2019;43</p></td>

<td><p>11&#x00BD;d.</p></td>

<td><p>6&#x00BD;d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1943-&#x2019;44</p></td>

<td><p>11&#x00BD;d.</p></td>

<td><p>6&#x00BD;d.</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<ol>

<li>(b) 20.5.1937 decreased from 6d. to 5d. 28.8.1940 increased from 5d. to 8d.; 3d. imposed.<br/>12.3.1941 increased from 8d. to 9d.; increased from 3d. to 4d.<br/>25.2.1942 increased from 9d. to 11&#x00BD;d.; increased from 4d. to 6&#x00BD;d.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) The rate of customs duty paid to the National Road Fund is determined in terms of Section 5 (1) (a) of Act 42 of 1935, as amended by Act 35 of 1936 and Act 50 of 1937, which reads:<br/>&#x201C;So much (not exceeding three pence) of the amount paid by way of customs duty (and not refunded or rebated) on every gallon of petrol imported into the Union since the thirty-first day of March, 1935, as exceeds two pence&#x201D;.<br/>In terms of this formula the rate on which payments to the National Road Fund are based, has remained constant at 3d. per gallon.</li>

<li>(ii) No payment in respect of excise duty is made to the National Road Fund.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(b) Nil.</li>

</ol>

</li>

</ol>

<p>(3)</p>

</tr>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Native Locations in Drakensberg</heading>

<question by="#christopher" to="#minister_of_native_affairs">

<num>XIV.</num>

<from>Mr. CHRISTOPHER (for Mr. Marwick)</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Native Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether his attention has been drawn to a press statement to the effect that the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry envisages the possibility of acquiring entirely new lands for native settlements by establishing new and independent native locations to avert soil erosion and overcrowding in the Drakensberg, Natal; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether the natives resident in the existing locations on the foothills of the Drakensberg, the control of which is vested in the Governor-General-in-Council by Letters Patent, dated 27th April, 1864, are to remain undisturbed in their occupation and possession of such areas; if not,</li>

<li>(3) what course is to be followed in obtaining from the natives the respresenttation of their views on this matter; and</li>

<li>(4) whether there was any consultation between the Minister of Native Affairs <span class="col_2619-2620" refersTo="page_1319"/>and the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry or with the natives in the Drakensberg area affected prior to the issue of the press statement referred to in (1).</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_native_affairs" to="#christopher">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIVE AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) The Inter-Departmental Committee appointed by the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry has been instructed to investigate the problem of soil deterioration in the Drakensberg Conservation area, and to make recommendations, and I regret that it is not possible to make a statement until the report of the Committee has been received and considered.</li>

<li>(3) The Committee will take such steps as may be necessary to obtain the views of the natives living the areas affected, should circumstances so require.</li>

<li>(4) (a) Yes. (b) No.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#ballinger" to="#minister_of_native_affairs">

<from>Mrs. BALLINGER:</from>

<p>Arising out of that reply, will the hon. Minister give us the terms of reference of his Departmental Committee?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_native_affairs" to="#ballinger">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIVE AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>It will be published. I have not a copy here, but if the hon. member likes, I will have a copy sent to her.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Employment Board</heading>

<question by="#christopher" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XV.</num>

<from>Mr. CHRISTOPHER (for Mr. Marwick)</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Labour:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he has appointed an Employment Board under the Soldiers and War Workers Employment Act, 1944; if so,</li>

<li>(2) what are the names of the members of the Board;</li>

<li>(3) whether the two senior organisations representing the men who have been on active service were excluded from nominating employees&#x2019; representatives; if so, why;</li>

<li>(4) whether he invited nominations from (a) the British Empire Service League and (b) the Moths Service Organisation; and, if not,</li>

<li>(5) whether he will now invite such organisations to nominate representatives.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#christopher">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) Brig. F. L. A. Buchanan, M.C., V.D. (Chairman).<br/>Captain L. E. Orkin.<br/>Captain J. White, M.C.<br/>Major W. A. Currie, M.C.<br/>Mr. H. G. Reid.<br/>Mr. J. Calder.<br/>Mr. S. Mann.<br/>Mr. R. Knevitt.<br/>Mr. P. J. Hodgson.</li>

<li>(3) It is presumed that the hon. member refers to the British Empire Service League and the Moths Service Organisation. These organisations were not asked to nominate employees&#x2019; representatives as they do not represent employees as such. Trade union organisations were invited to nominate employee representatives.</li>

<li>(4) (a) and (b) Yes.</li>

<li>(5) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Use of Meat in East Coast Fever Areas</heading>

<question by="#steenkamp" to="#minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<num>XVI.</num>

<from>Dr. STEENKAMP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<block name="quote">What amount has been recovered by the Government from the use and sale of meat derived from cattle slaughtered by the Government in Vryheid and surrounding districts since the outbreak of East Coast Fever to date.</block>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry" to="#steenkamp">

<from>The MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FORESTRY:</from>

<p>The gross receipts were &#x00A3;57,914 19s. 3d., but the total expenditure amounted to &#x00A3;66,749 13s., including the costs incurred in connection with the scheme.</p>

</answer>

<question by="#strydom" to="#minister">

<num>XVII.</num>

<from>Mr. J. G. STRYDOM</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

<question by="#swart" to="#minister">

<num>XVIII.</num>

<from>Mr. SWART</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Price Control: Charges Against Inspector</heading>

<question by="#latimer" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XIX.</num>

<from>Mr. LATIMER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether an inspector from the office of the Price Controller, Pretoria, appeared in the Pretoria magistrate&#x2019;s court on or about 16th February, 1945, in a preparatory examination on eight counts of extortion or, alternatively, bribery;</li>

<li><span class="col_2621-2622" refersTo="page_1320"/>(2) whether the magistrate refused the Press permission to publish the name of the accused, the charges preferred against him and details of the preparatory, examination; if so,</li>

<li>(3) whether he will state the reasons for such refusal; if not, why not; and</li>

<li>(4) whether he will take steps to make these facts available for publication.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#latimer">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes, on the 16th February, 1945.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.</li>

<li>(3) and (4) The magistrate misread Section 69 (5) of the Criminal Procedure and Evidence Act, 1917, and excluded the Press in error. The matter was rectified and Press allowed in court at the adjourned hearing on the 19th February, 1945.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways : Accident at Pinedene Crossing</heading>

<question by="#van_onselen" to="#minister_of_transport">

<num>XX.</num>

<from>Mr. VAN ONSELEN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Transport:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether en enquiry has been held on the cause of the railway accident at Pinedene crossing on the 25th December, 1944, when six persons lost their lives; if so,</li>

<li>(2) what was the result of such enquiry; and</li>

<li>(3) what does the Minister intend doing to prevent any further accidents at this crossing.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport" to="#van_onselen">

<from>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) and (2) A departmental enquiry was held, but it is not the practice to make public the reports of such enquiries.</li>

<li>(3) The crossing is protected by standard halt boards and crossing signs. As the amount of road traffic using the crossing is not heavy, it is not considered that the provision of additional protection is justified.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#erasmus" to="#minister">

<num>XXI.</num>

<from>Mr. F. C. ERASMUS</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

<question by="#de_wet" to="#minister">

<num>XXII.</num>

<from>Mr. H. C. DE WET</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Demobilisation and War in the Far East</heading>

<question by="#le_roux" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XXIII.</num>

<from>Mr. J. N. LE ROUX</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) To what extent will the naval and air forces be committed in the Far East;</li>

<li>(2) whether members of the land forces will be demobilised after the end of hostilities in Europe; and</li>

<li>(3) whether members of the naval and air forces who do not volunteer will be demobilised at the end of hostilities in Europe.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#le_roux">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) No decision has yet been made.</li>

<li>(2) Yes, except those required for the continuation of the war against Japan.</li>

<li>(3) Yes, unless they are required for further service in terms of their existing contracts.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister">

<num>XXIV.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Report of Kakamas Commission of Enquiry</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_lands">

<num>XXV.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Lands:</p>

<block name="quote">Whether the Kakamas Commission of Enquiry has completed its report; if so, whether he will lay it upon the Table; and, if not, whether he will take steps to expedite the completion of the report.</block>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS:</from>

<p>Yes. I intend laying the report on the Table immediately after receiving it from the printers.</p>

</answer>

<question by="#sullivan" to="#minister">

<num>XXVI.</num>

<from>Mr. SULLIVAN</from>

<p>&#x2014;Reply standing over.</p>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Meat Graders</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF AGRICULTURE AND FORESTRY replied to Question No. XVII by Mr. J. G. Strydom standing over from 23rd February:</p>

<question by="#strydom" to="#minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) What is the average number of meat graders employed in Pretoria, Germiton, Johannesburg, Durban, Pietermaritzburg, Bloemfontein, East London, Port Elizabeth, Kimberley and Cape Town, respectively;</li>

<li>(2) what was the maximum number of meat graders employed at these various places at any time during the periods 1st May, 1942, to 30th April, 1943, and 1st May, 1943, to 30th April, 1944 respectively;</li>

<li>(3) what training did such graders receive;</li>

<li>(4) what was the maximum number of meat graders employed at any time in connection with the export of meat during each of the years 1937, 1938 and 1939 at Johannesburg, Durban and East London, respectively;</li>

<li><span class="col_2625-2626" refersTo="page_1322"/>(5) how many cattle and sheep, respectively, were graded at each of these three places during each such year;</li>

<li>(6) what was the salary scale of such graders; and</li>

<li>(7) (a) what training did such graders receive and (b) what was the average duration of their training.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry" to="#strydom">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

</tr>

<td><p>3</p></td>

</table>

<p>In addition to this number of meat graders, 1 is used for co-ordination and 1 for relief purposes, while 2 are assisting the Food Control Organisation at the places mentioned and other places. The number of graders who are being trained, has not been included.</p>

<li>(4) No graders were employed in a fulltime capacity in connection with the export of meat. Export took place in a sporadic manner, and a few officers devoted only a part of their time to this work when export consignments became available.</li>

<ol>

<li>(6) As is evident from the reply to (4), it is not possible to indicate which portion of the salaries of the officers can be allocated to their work in connection with meat grading.</li>

<li>(7) (a) and (b) The officers concerned possess at least the B.Sc, degree in Agriculture, which is a four years&#x2019; university course, and apart from the knowledge acquired in connection with the study of Animal Husbandry as part of that course, those who did not study meat grading overseas, received a few months&#x2019; practical training locally.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Polish Children in Camp Near Oudtshoorn</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF WELFARE AND DEMOBILISATION replied to Question No. XVII by Mr. Brink, standing over from 23rd February :</p>

<question by="#brink" to="#minister_of_welfare_and_demobilisation">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether a number of Polish children are housed in a camp in the vicinity of Oudtshoorn; if so, (a) how many, (b) why were they placed there, (c) whether they came from slum areas in Poland, (d) whether some of them wore Polish military clothes and (e) whether their clothes were disinfected before entering the Union;</li>

<li>(2) whether an outbreak of poliomyelitis (infantile paralysis) occurred in the vicinity of the camp; and</li>

<li>(3) whether his Department took steps to ensure that they were not carriers of poliomyelitis; and, if so, what steps.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_welfare_and_demobilisation" to="#brink">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) 300.</li>

<li>(b) They are refugees who were allowed into the Union on a temporary permit for the duration of the war. They were housed in a camp which was available at <span class="col_2627-2628" refersTo="page_1323"/>Oudtshoorn at the time, staffed mainly by Polish personnel, because they could not, on account of language difficulties, be placed with private families.</li>

<li>(c) The majority resided in rural areas in Poland.</li>

<li>(d) The Department of Social Welfare has no knowledge of this.</li>

<li>(e) The usual precautions prescribed by the Health Regulations were taken when the children entered the Union.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(2) One case has occurred in the municipal area and one in the divisional council area of Oudtshoorn.</li>

<li>(3) No special measures, beyond the normal precautions referred to in (1) (e), were taken as the mode of spread of poliomyelitis is unknown to medical science at present. In any case, the children arrived in the Union early in 1943, about eighteen months before the present outbreak of poliomyelitis began.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Assault on Member of Parliament in Johannesburg</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. VIII by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many persons were arrested in connection with an assault on a member of the House of Assembly in Johannesburg last year and what are their names;</li>

<li>(2) what are the names of those who were prosecuted;</li>

<li>(3) whether any of the others were interned; if so, who;</li>

<li>(4) whether any of them were again released; if so, who;</li>

<li>(5) whether any of the persons arrested became Crown witnesses; if so, who;</li>

<li>(6) whether any of the persons arrested made a statement to the police; if so,</li>

<li>(7) whether he will lay such statement upon the Table; if not, whether he will allow members of Parliament to inspect such statement;</li>

<li>(8) whether the member who had been assaulted requested the Crown for permission to see such statement; if so, whether he was granted permission; and</li>

<li>(9) whether any of the persons arrested were subpoenaed to give evidence; if so, whether any of them gave evidence.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Fourteen. (1) Gabriel Vorster, (2) W. S. Pretorius, (3) J. H. Jordaan, (4) J. A. J. B. Vorster, (5) F. P. Cornelius, (6) H. J. van der Berg, (7) G. H. du Toit, (8) J. Seyffert, (9) P. J. Roets, (10) A. J. Benade, (11) C. van der Westhuizen, (12) L. Unterhorst, (13) L. H. Mostert and (14) H. W. Kameyer.</li>

<li>(2) No. (1) Gabriel Vorster was prosecuted in the Supreme Court for the assault and convicted. Nos. (2), (3), (4), (5), (6) and (7) were prosecuted for other offences disclosed in the investigation. Nos. (2), (3) and (4) were convicted and Nos. (5), (6) and (7) were acquitted.</li>

<li>(3) Yes. Nos. (2) to (14) inclusive.</li>

<li>(4) Yes. Nos. (10) to (14) inclusive.</li>

<li>(5) Yes. No. (11).</li>

<li>(6) Yes.</li>

<li>(7) No. Statements made to the police during the investigation of a criminal charge are privileged,</li>

<li>(8) The complainant asked to be allowed to see the statement. This was refused on the ground that the statement was confidential and privileged.</li>

<li>(9) Yes. No. (11) gave evidence.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways : Molestations in Suburban Subways</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT replied to Question No. XII by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_transport">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<p>Whether he will issue instructions that steps be taken to provide protection for women and girls against molestation and jostling when passing through the subways at Woodstock and Salt River railway stations.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<p>I am not aware of any such molestation or jostling. Constables are, however, in attendance at both stations.</p>

<p><span class="col_2629-2630" refersTo="page_1324"/>Owing, principally, to the large number of persons engaged on the construction of the new graving dock, congestion does at times take place in the subway at Woodstock, and in order to ease the position certain improvements have been taken in hand.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Gambling Devices</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XIII by Dr. Van Nierop standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the prohibition of gambling devices in public places is still in force; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether it has been brought to his notice that such devices are again being offered to the public in Cape Town and other places for amusement; and, if so,</li>

<li>(3) whether he will take the necessary steps to put a final stop to the use of such devices.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) and (3) Whenever information is received that such devices are being operated, action is taken by the police. There have been four prosecutions in the Cape Peninsula in the last twelve months.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_economic_development" to="#marwick">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Yes.</li>

<li>(b) No.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(b) &#x00A3;90 per mensem.</li>

<heading>Euphorbia and Landolphia Rubber: Research</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT replied to Question No. XVI by Mr. Marwick standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_economic_development">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) What was the expenditure incurred on experiments on euphorbia and landolphia rubber;</li>

<li>(2) what were (a) the names and (b) the qualifications, if any, of the persons responsible for these experiments; and</li>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_economic_development" to="#marwick">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Euphorbia rubber: &#x00A3;4,263 19 2 Landolphia rubber: &#x00A3;58,540 2 7</li>

<li>(2) Originally the experiments were directed by a Sub-Committee of the War Time Research Committee of the <span class="col_2631-2632" refersTo="page_1325"/>Department of Commerce and Industries, consisting of:&#x2014;<br/>(a) Names. (b) Qualifications.<br/>Dr. F. J. Tromp: Director of War Time Research Committee and Fuel Research Institute, Pretoria.<br/>Mr. N. Eckbo: Director of Forestry Research Station, Pretoria.<br/>Dr. R. A. Dyer: Department of Botany and Plant Pathology, Pretoria.<br/>Mr. V. Cutler : Chemical Division, Department of Agriculture and Forestry, Pretoria. <br/>Mr. F. Britten: Director, Government Chemical Laboratories, Johannesburg.<br/>Mr. C. B. Young: Department of Native Affairs, Pretoria.<br/>Mr. F. P. Bovet: Representative of the Controller of Rubber.<br/>As from 1st September, 1943, the direction was taken over by the Rubber Production. Section, under the direction of the Controller of Rubber. The staff of this Section consisted of:&#x2014;<br/>(a) Names. (b) Qualifications.<br/>Headquarters.<br/>Director: Mr. A. V. Ames: Director, Dunlop Plantations, Malaya, and Ministry of Supply Representative.<br/>Deputy Director: Mr. F. P; Bovet: Director, Revertex Limited, London, and Technical Adviser to the Controller of Rubber.<br/>Production Manager: Captain A. E. Brooker: Manager, Socfin Rubber Estates, Malaya, and Technical Adviser to the Controller of Rubber.<br/>Accountant: Mr. C. G. Johnstone: Chartered Accountant.<br/>Euphorbia Research.<br/>Prof. F. Warren: Professor of Chemistry, Natal University College, Pietermaritzburg.<br/>Prof. A. W. Bayer : Professor of Botany, Natal University College, Pietermaritzburg.<br/>Mr. T. MacAlpine: Rubber Planter, Malaya.<br/>Mr. O. West: Pasture Research Officer.<br/>Mr. E. W. Fogg: Chemist.<br/>Mr. A. White: Chemical Engineer.<br/>Mr. A. Davidson: Engineer, Natal Sugar Estates Ltd., Mount Edgecombe.<br/>Mr. L. I. Goldblatt: Chemist, Government Chemical Laboratory, Johannesburg. <br/>Mr. F. Valentin: Chemist War Time Research.<br/>Mr. J. Antonowitz: Analytical Chemist. Technical Staff, Dunlop (S.A.) Ltd in advisory capacity.<br/>Landolphia Research.<br/>Prof. L. Taverner: Director, Minerals Research Laboratory, Wit waterstrand University, Johannesburg.<br/>Mr. J. Laschinger : Of the staff of the Minerals Research Laboratory, Witwatersrand University, Johannesburg.<br/>Mr. R. W. Burnand: Of the firm of Head Wrightson (S.A.) Ltd., Johannesburg.</li>

<li>(3)

<ol>

<li>(a) Commercial production of rubber was not reached at the time operations were closed.</li>

<li>(b) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Advisory Committes to Controllers</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT replied to Question No. XVII by Mr. Marwick, standing over from 27th February :</p>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_economic_development">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether advisory committees to controllers are composed only of representatives of the large firms in an industry; if so,</li>

<li>(2) what steps, if any, are taken to prevent members of such advisory committees from obtaining information about the productivity and financial status of their trade rivals;</li>

<li>(3) whether the return to be rendered on form No. 7, as amended, to the Controller of Textiles requires every firm engaged in the textile industry, under penalty, to state in respect of 1944 (a) the gross value of all materials used, (b) total wages and salaries paid, (c) other non-capital expenditure and (d) gross sales of factory;</li>

<li>(4) whether a form in use by the rubber control known as &#x201C;Application to Purchase. Mechanical Rubber Goods&#x201D; requires the applicant to supply the name of the consumer, his address and precise details of the use to which the goods will be put and to append the consumer&#x2019;s signature; if so,</li>

<li>(5) whether he will prohibit such details being rendered to a committee constituted as in (1);</li>

<li><span class="col_2633-2634" refersTo="page_1326"/>(6) whether the Controller of Textiles called for the particulars specified in form No. 7 during the years 1942 and 1943;</li>

<li>(7) whether he will impose such severe penalties as will deter persons from making improper use of the particulars required to be furnished; and</li>

<li>(8) whether he will take further steps to prevent all possibility of misuse by persons coming into possession of the information so furnished.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_economic_development" to="#marwick">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) Falls away.</li>

<li>(3) Yes.</li>

<li>(4) Yes.</li>

<li>(5) The commitees act in an advisory capacity only and have no access to individual returns rendered to the Controllers.</li>

<li>(6) A return similar to the current form No. 7 was called for under Government Notice No. 2528 dated 4th December, 1942.</li>

<li>(7) All information furnished to Controllers is treated as strictly confidential and in this connection the hon. member&#x2019;s attention is invited to the penalties provided for in Proclamation No. 319 of 1942 (War Measure No. 146 of 1942), which are considered to be adequate.</li>

<li>(8) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>South-West Africa : Publication of Newspapers</heading>

<p>The PRIME MINISTER replied to Question No. XXV by Mr. Klopper standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#klopper" to="#prime_minister">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Which printing firms in South-West Africa were allowed to publish newspapers in that territory during the period from September, 1939, to December, 1944;</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) in which language were such news papers required to be published,</li>

<li>(b) how many copies of each paper were allowed to be printed,</li>

<li>(c) what are the names of the editors, and</li>

<li>(d) what are the names and addresses of&#x2014;

<ol>

<li>(1) the owners and</li>

<li>(ii) the directors of such newspapers and printing works; and</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(3) which of such persons are (a) Union nationals by birth (b) Union nationals by naturalisation, (c) nationals of other countries and (d) nationals of states with which the Union is at war.</li>

</ol>

</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#prime_minister" to="#klopper">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) and (2) (a) and (b) Except in regard to the quantity of paper used there has been no question of &#x201C;allowing&#x201D; either the publication of newspapers in South-West Africa of the number of copies to be printed, and no control was exercised over the language employed as a medium. Messrs. John Meinert, Limited, Windhoek, published the following papers prior to September, 1939, in the language and quantities indicated in each case and have continued to do so since:<br/>The &#x201C;Windhoek Advertiser&#x201D; in English&#x2014;1,000 copies.<br/><span class="col_2635-2636" refersTo="page_1327"/><br/>The Suidwes Afrikaner&#x201D; in Afrikaans 1,400 copies.<br/>The &#x201C;Allgemeine Zeitung&#x201D; (formerly the &#x201C;Deutsche Beobachter&#x201D;) in German&#x2014;2,700 copies.</li>

<li>(2) (c) The &#x201C;Windhoek Advertiser&#x201D;&#x2014;Mr. J. D. Lardner-Burke at present on active service and Mr. A. Harris is acting;<br/>The &#x201C;Suidwes Afrikaner&#x201D;&#x2014;Mr. L. C. Talj aard;<br/>The &#x201C;Allgemiene Zeitung&#x201D;&#x2014;Mr. John Meinert.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(d)

<ol>

<li>(i) The owners of the &#x201C;Windhoek Advertiser&#x201D; and the &#x201C;Suidwes Afrikaner&#x201D; are Messrs. John Meinert (Proprietary) Limited, Windhoek, and of the &#x201C;Allgemeine Zeitung&#x201D; Deutsche Verlag (Proprietary) Limited, Windhoek;</li>

<li>(ii) The director of the &#x201C;Windhoek Advertiser&#x201D; and the &#x201C;Suidwes Afrikaner&#x201D; is Mr. John Meinert, Windhoek, and of the &#x201C;Allgemeine Zeitung&#x201D; Mr. John Meinert is managing director; other directors are Messrs. Carl List, Heiner Meinert and Gerhard Voigts, all of Windhoek;</li>

</ol>

</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(3)

<ol>

<li>(a) Messrs. J. D. Lardner-Burke and L. C. Taljaard;</li>

<li>(b) Mr. A. Harris who is a Union national by domicile;</li>

<li>(c) and (d) The remaining persons referred to in (2) (c) and (d) are enemy nationals.</li>

</ol>

</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>South-West Africa : Delimitation</heading>

<p>The PRIME MINISTER replied to Question No. XXVI by Mr. Klopper standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#klopper" to="#prime_minister">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether there has been a delimitation of electoral divisions in South-West Africa during the past 13 years; if so, when; if not,</li>

<li>(2) (a) when was the last delimitation and (b) why was it decided to hold a general election of members of the Legislative Assembly before a delimitation of electoral divisions had taken place;</li>

<li>(3) whether the Union Government as mandatory, intends allowing a general election to be held in South-West Africa without a prior delimitation of electoral divisions; and</li>

<li>(4) how many voters have been disfranchised by legislation since 1st September, 1939, in each of the electoral divisions in South-West Africa.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#prime_minister" to="#klopper">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) December, 1931.</li>

<li>(b) Special provisions, unlike those applicable in the Union, exist in South-West Africa regarding delimitation. Under the conditions now obtaining in the Mandated Territory and with due regard to the fact that the life of the present Legislative Assembly expires on 25th March, 1945, the Advisory Council advised against a new delimitation. This advice was accepted.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(3) Yes.</li>

<li>(4) Gibeon 178 Gobabis 309, Grootfontein 561, Keetmanshoop 147, Luderitz 438, Okahandja 448, Otjimwarongo 330 Stampriet 96, Swakopmund 505, Warmbad 34, Windhoek District 356, Windhoek Central 589.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Legislative Assembly of South-West Africa : Union Government Nominees</heading>

<question by="#klopper" to="#prime_minister">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) What are the names of the persons (a) appointed by the Union Government as members of the Legislative Assembly of South-West Africa after the recent general election held there and (b) nominated at the time by the Administrator of South-West Africa for appointment; and</li>

<li>(2) what are the names of the members of the Advisory Council in South-West Africa who have been appointed from time to time since 1st January, 1933.</li>

</question>

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) The Governor-General-in-Council, in terms of Section 13 (2) of Act No. 42 of 1925 granted approval on the 1st March, 1940, for the appoint <span class="col_2637-2638" refersTo="page_1328"/>ment by the Administrator of South-West Africa, of the following persons as nominated members of the Legislative Assembly of South-West Africa:<br/>Mr. D. W. F. E. Ballot. Mr. G. P. Kirsten.<br/>Mr. J. D Lardner-Burke.<br/>Mr. E. Sander.<br/>Mr. J. F. Schroeder, Mr. L. J. F, von Maltitz.</li>

</li>

</li>

<li>(2) Mr. F. H. Klette&#x2014;1 July, 1933 (in the place of Mr. H. G. Scott (transferred).<br/>Mr. L. C. F. Taljaard&#x2014;1 June, 1934 (in the place of Mr. P. Mueller, resigned).<br/>Mr. H. Hoerlein&#x2014;28th November, 1934.<br/>Mr. L. J. F. von Maltitz&#x2014;28th November, 1934 Mr. D. I. H. R. Meintjes&#x2014;28th November, 1934.<br/>Mr. E. F. Oettle&#x2014;15th May, 1936 (in the place of Mr. D. I. H. R. Meintjes, resigned).<br/>Mr. A. J. Rossouw, 30th May, 1939 (in the place of Mr. E. F. Oettle, resigned).<br/>Mr. J. C. Jooste&#x2014;1st March, 1940.<br/>Mr. A. J. Rossouw&#x2014;1st March, 1940.<br/>Mr. J. F. Schroeder&#x2014;1st March, 1940.<br/>Mr. A. A. le Roux&#x2014;17th December, 1941 (in the place of Mr. A. J. Rossouw, resigned).<br/>Mr. E. Dahl&#x2014;25th February, 1944 (in the place of Mr. J. F. Schroeder, deceased).<br/>Mr. C. G. C. Clarke&#x2014;6th October, 1944 (in the place of Mr. E. Dahl, deceased).</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<heading>Chief of Railway Police</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT replied to Question No. XXXII by Mr. Klopper, standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#klopper" to="#minister_of_transport">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Who has been appointed Chief of Railway Police and what was his former rank;</li>

<li>(2) whether he acted as Chief of Police prior to his appointment; if so, (a) for what period and (b) whether compaints were received about the manner in which he performed his duties;</li>

<li>(3) whether he has been appointed for a probationary period; if so, what period has the Administration fixed for his probation;</li>

<li>(4) whether the period was longer than the ordinary period; if so, why;</li>

<li>(5) whether a similar period was required in the case of other officials; if so, what are their names;</li>

<li>(6) what was the probationary period of (a) the former Chief of Police and (b) the present Public Relations Officer;</li>

<li>(7) whether the former Chief of Police had any experience prior to his appointment;</li>

<li>(8) (a) what were the salaries of the respective Chiefs of Police in the past and (b) what is the salary in respect of the present appointment;</li>

<li>(9) whether an Assistant Chief Police Officer has been appointed; if not, why not; and</li>

<li>(10) how long has the post been vacant.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport" to="#klopper">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) H. J. T. Venter, who was previously Deputy Chief of Police and Investigation.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) From 26th January, 1944, to 30th June, 1944.</li>

<li>(b) I am not prepared to furnish personal information of this nature concerning any senior officer.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(3) Yes, a probationary period of twelve months.</li>

<li><span class="col_2639-2640" refersTo="page_1329"/>(4) and (5) No specific probationary period has been laid down for application in cases of promotion. This case was regarded as exceptional and the customary probationary period of three months was extended to twelve months.</li>

<li>(6)

<ol>

<li>(a) Three months.</li>

<li>(b) Three months, in addition to eight months&#x2019; occupation of the post in a casual capacity prior to appointment to the regular staff.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(7) Yes.</li>

<li>(8)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) Initially &#x00A3;1,000, then &#x00A3;1,050, &#x00A3;1,100 and &#x00A3;1,200.</li>

<li>(ii) &#x00A3;1,500.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(b) &#x00A3;1,400.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(9) An appointment has been made in an acting capacity, and the filling of the post is in hand.</li>

<li>(10) Since 1st, July, 1944.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>South-West Africa: Control of Police Force</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XXXIII by Mr. Klopper, standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#klopper" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) When was the control and authority over the police force in South-West Africa transferred to the Union Government and (b) what were the reasons for such transfer;</li>

<li>(2) (a) what annual amounts have been paid by the Union Government to the police force in South-West Africa since such transfer and (b) what amounts has the Union Government claimed from the Administration;</li>

<li>(3) what were the recommendations of the various Administrators in connection therewith; and</li>

<li>(4) whether he will make a statement on the Union Government&#x2019;s intentions and policy in connection with this matter.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#klopper">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) As from 1st June, 1939.</li>

<li>(b) It was considered to be in the interests both of the Union and of the Mandated Territory of South-West Africa.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) and (b) This amount is not exactly ascertainable since, from the date of incorporation, the cost of policing South-West Africa has been included in that of the Union as a whole. In terms of the agreement come to at the time of incorporation, the Administration of the Mandated Territory pays the Union Government &#x00A3;114,000 per annum for such policing and in respect of the pensions of members of the South-West Africa Police taken over by the South African Police.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(3) The agreement which forms the basis of the Police (South-West Africa) Act, 1939, was made between the Union Government and the South-West Africa Admininistration and it is presumed that the Administrator holding office at the time concurred therein.</li>

<li>(4) At this juncture such a statement would serve no useful purpose.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Police : Special Allowance for Parliamentary Duties</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XXXV by Mr. Swart standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#swart" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether all members of the police force who have been detached for duty in connection with the Parliamentary Session and are normally stationed outside Cape Town are receiving the special allowance in accordance with the scale laid down by Government Notice No. 209 of the 7th February, 1941, published in Government Gazette No. 2853; and, if not,</li>

<li>(2) what allowances do they receive.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#swart">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Members of the Force who are specially transferred to Cape Town temporarily for duty in connection with the Parliamentary session will be dealt with in terms of South African Police Regulation No. 16 (4) as contained in Government Notice No. 209 of the 7th February, 1941, and instructions have been issued to this effect. These instructions will, however, not affect temporary transfers of members of the Force necessitated by seasonal or other circumstances for duty in Cape Town, Durban, Johannesburg or any other place in the Union. Such members will be dealt with in terms of Regulation 16 (1), (2) and (3) as has been customary throughout the Force in the past.</li>

<li><span class="col_2641-2642" refersTo="page_1330"/>(2) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#swart" to="#minister_of_justice">

<from>Mr. SWART:</from>

<p>Arising but of the Minister&#x2019;s reply which just contains a number of figures, would he please state clearly whether the police now get the allowance which they complain they were hot getting; just as other public servants do.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#swart">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<p>It will be too long to say it now I will tell it personally to the hon. member and then he can put the question again if he so desires.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Resignations of Magistrates</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XXXVI by Dr. D&#x00F6;nges standing over from 27 th February:</p>

<question by="#d&#x00f6;nges" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many magistrates (a) left the service during each of the years from 1940 to 1944 and (b) are expected to leave the service during each of the years from 1945 to 1950 through superannuations; and</li>

<li>(2) whether he intends taking any steps to expedite promotion to the grade of magistrate.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#d&#x00f6;nges">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) In 1940, 14 retired; in 1941, 17 retired and one died; in 1942, 15 retired, one died and one resigned; in 1943, 15 retired and two died; in 1944, 14 retired and two died.</li>

<li>(b) 1945, nine; 1946, four; 1947, four; 1948, five; 1949, eight; and 1950., ten.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(2) The Department has made certain suggestions to the Public Service Commission of Enquiry which, if adopted, may alleviate the position.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Broadcasting: English, Commentators in Durban and Johannesburg</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT replied to Question No. XXXVIII by Mr. Marwick, standing over from 27th February:</p>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_economic_development">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the English commentators in Durban have received notice of the termination of their services; if so,</li>

<li>(2) (a) what are the names of the persons whose services have been discontinued and (b) from what date are they to cease broadcasting;</li>

<li>(3) what are the grounds for terminating the services of the English commenta tors at (a) Johannesburg and (b) Durban</li>

<li>(4) what appointments have been made in the places of the English commentators referred to in (3);</li>

<li>(5) whether the Minister will agree to a ballot being held among the licensed listeners in the Transvaal and Natal to ascertain whether the majority of listeners are for or against (a) the retention of the English news commentators concerned and (b) the dismissal of the present Board of Governors of the South African Broadcasting Corporation; and, if so,</li>

<li>(6) whether the Minister will facilitate the holding of such a ballot the cost of providing the paper and printing of the ballot papers arid of labour having been guaranteed</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_economic_development" to="#marwick">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) M. Broughton.</li>

<li>(b) Hot yet decided.</li>

</ol>

</li>

<li>(3) The Board of Governors of the South African Broadcasting Corporation in its discretion decided to make a change.</li>

<li>(4) (a) W. K. Buchanan and (b) an appointment has not yet been made.</li>

<li>(5) Ho.</li>

<li>(6) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#explosion_in_ammunition_factory">

<heading>EXPLOSION IN AMMUNITION FACTORY</heading>

<speech by="#prime_minister">

<from>*THE <person refersTo="hansard_za">PRIME MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>With leave, I would like to make a further statement on the subject of the explosion which occurred in the ammunition factory in Pretoria on the, 1st March. Yesterday a question was put here in connection with the terrible accident. I would like to inform the House that the latest figures are not available. The casualties which have been ascertained are altogether 31., That includes Europeans, 4 men and 11 women&#x2014;total 15; non-Europeans 16, which brings the total to 31. I may add that there exists a great deal of uncertainty regarding the cause of the accident. That uncertainty continues to exist. In the meantime all the Departments concerned are instituting the necessary enquiries in order to get evidence whilst the facts are still fresh in mind, so that a thorough investigation may take place as soon as possible.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#rand_water_board_statutes_1903-&#x2019;44_amendment_private_bill">

<heading><span class="col_2643-2644" refersTo="page_1331"/>RAND WATER BOARD STATUTES 1903-&#x2019;44 AMENDMENT (PRIVATE) BILL</heading>

<p>First Order read : Second reading, Rand Water Board Statutes 1903-&#x2019;44 Amendment (Private) Bill.</p>

<speech by="#stratford">

<from>&#x2020;Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">STRATFORD</person>:</from>

<p>I move&#x2014;</p>

<block name="quote">That the Bill be read a second time.</block>

<p>In moving the second reading of this Bill I have to mention at the outset that the affairs of the Rand Water Board have been before this House on many occasions in the past, so I do not think it is necessary to explain in detail to the House what the Rand Water Board is, what its purposes are, or what its development has been during the past 40 odd years. I think it is sufficient for the purpose of introducing this Bill that I should remind the House that the Rand Water Board is a public utility non-profit making corporation formed in 1903 for the purpose of providing and supplying water to the Witwatersrand and neighbouring areas. Over the past 40 odd years the development and growth of the Rand Water Board&#x2019;s undertaking has run parallel to the development and growth of the Witwatersrand itself; and as time has gone on, and as the problems of the board have changed, it has been found necessary to introduce amendments to the original empowering ordinance of 1903. Such amendments have been introduced not only during the provincial stage, but since Union, and the result is a body of law which is generally known as the Rand Water Board Statutes. During the last session of Parliament a Bill was introduced into this House for the purpose mainly of extending the operations of the board to the Pretoria and Vereeniging areas, and it was originally intended that the provisions of today&#x2019;s Bill should be incorporated in the Bill that was introduced last session. Unfortunately, sir, delays took place in negotiations with Government departments which were prolonged to such an extent that it was not possible to give the necessary statutory notice in time to incorporate the provisions of this Bill into last year&#x2019;s Bill. So it has been necessary to frame a separate Bill to meet those difficulties. I propose to explain very shortly to the House what the purposes of the present Bill are. In the first place, let me say that they relate exclusively to the financial provisions of the Rand Water Board Statutes. As I have already mentioned, the board is a non-profit making undertaking, with the result that the manner in which the board is able to acquire funds, and the manner it disposes of its funds, are laid down within the four comers of the statutes. The revenue of the board is derived partly from loan funds, and partly from a charge for water to the various consumers. The great majority of the consumers comprise the various constituent municipalities, the Railway Administration and the Chamber of Mines. They, in fact, represent 96 per cent. of the total number of consumers of water sold by the board. As I say, the manner in which the board is empowered to deal with this money is laid down within the four corners of the statutes, and these statutes provide that any surplus of revenue over expenditure must be returned to the consumers, or I should put it, must accrue to the immediate benefit of consumers save where the statutes make special provision for the creation of reserve funds. The purpose of this Bill is to introduce certain modifications in the present powers of the board in relation to those reserve funds. The first amendment relates to the board&#x2019;s power to set aside an ordinary reserve fund. This power was originally conferred on the board in 1904, and enables the board to set aside annually from moneys received from the sale of water a fund to meet contingencies, to equalise the charges for water and to cover maintenance maintenance of the board&#x2019;s works. The present position is that the amount that can be set aside is subject to the approval of the Governor-General, but this limitation upon the board&#x2019;s power has been found to be extremely inconvenient. In the nature of things, as I am sure the House will appreciate, it takes perhaps two months from the time application is made to the Governor-General for his approval until that approval is granted; the result of that has been that at least two months before the end of its financial year the board has been obliged to survey the result of its year&#x2019;s operations, and to consider what its surplus is likely to be, and in what way it should dispose of its surplus, before it really knows where it stands financially, before it really knows what the surpluses are going to be, or what ought to be done with them. That has proved very inconvenient, so the purpose of the first amendment is to dispense with this requirement of obtaining the Governor-General&#x2019;s sanction for an appropriation to the reserve fund, and to put in its place an automatic limitation of the amount that can be so set aside. The House will, I am sure, appreciate that the sole purpose so far as one can judge, of this provision by which the Governor-General&#x2019;s approval has to be sought, is to prevent the board from burdening the present generation by creating very large reserves, unnecessarily large reserves. In such circumstances, the Governor-General one may suppose, could step in and object to the appropriation of so large a sum to the reserve fund. In asking the House to pass this amendment, by which the Governor-General&#x2019;s approval <span class="col_2645-2646" refersTo="page_1332"/>is to be dispensed with, we insert in place of that an automatic limitation upon the amount which can be set aside for reserve in any one year. Let me also mention, Mr. Speaker, that all the financial powers of the board are subject to the scrutiny of the board&#x2019;s auditors, who are appointed by the Government and have very wide powers. They have power to make recommendations and to draw attention to any misuse of the board&#x2019;s funds which they consider should be rectified; so that provides an additional check on any inclination the board may have to act in an improper or unwise way in relation to its funds. The next provision, which I think is the most important provision of the Bill, relates to the setting aside of moneys to cover depreciation and renewals. The present position is that the board, in arriving at a charge for water, which is known as the uniform rate, may make provision for depreciation, but the board has been advised that under the statutes as they stand at present they can only make allowance for depreciation in respect of items which are actually valued, depreciated and written off. In the nature of things the board, with very considerable assets of various kinds, has found it impossible to embark on a valuation of its assets annually for the purpose of making this appropriate allowance for depreciation and the purpose of this amendment is therefore to create an appropriate fund to cover depreciation and renewals into which the board may from time to time at its discretion place such sums as are considered necessary to cover those two items. Finally, I come to the provisions in the third reserve fund, called the Betterment Fund. This provision, Mr. Speaker, is of a very minor character, and the only changes which the present Bill seeks to introduce are as follows. In the first place, the Betterment Fund (which is already in existence) as its name suggests, was created for the purpose of providing for the type of expenditure to improve the assets of the board which falls midway between the capital form of expenditure and the revenue form of expenditure. It partakes of the character of both, and because of its rather ambiguous character difficulties have cropped up in the past as to whether a particular item could be appropriately called a betterment item or whether it fell into the category of &#x201C;revenue&#x201D; or &#x201C;capital.&#x201D; The purpose of this amendment is to enable the board in respect of its Betterment Fund to use it not only for betterment items but also for items which are appropriately describable as capital expenditure. That will meet the difficulties the board has experienced in the past. The only other amendment in this regard is of a very minor nature, and enables the board instead of setting a limitation of 1d. per 1,000 gallons upon this Betterment Fund the limitation is now set at a sixth of the uniform rate. The uniform rate is at present 6d. A 1d. per 1,000 gallons has, in the past, represented one-sixth of the uniform rate. But the purpose of this amendment is to make allowance for the possibility that the uniform rate may either decline or be increased, in which case the 1d. per 1,000 gallons would be either too little or too much whereas the sixth will put the two into an appropriate relationship. I think that deals with Clauses 1, 2, 4, 5 and 7 of the Bill. Sections 3 and 8 of the Bill I do not think call for any comment at this stage at all events. With regard to Section 9, whereas the previous sections have made provision for the maximum amount which may be put to any of these funds I have described, Section 9 contains an overriding provision which limits the maximum sum which may in any one year be appropriated to the three funds jointly. This provision provides an additional check upon any inclination on the part of the board to make undue provision for the future at the expense of the present. In conclusion, I would like to remark generally that when I speak of the board I am speaking of a body which is in every respect representative of consumers; in other words, the board really comprises all the people who might conceivably be interested in this Bill. In so far that queries may be raised as to the effect of any of these provisions on the interests of these consumers, it is obviously important to remember that the consumers themselves subscribed to these amendments as conforming to their conception of the way in which the board&#x2019;s finances should be conducted.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from>Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>Is that in the evidence?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#stratford">

<from>&#x2020;Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">STRATFORD</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I am just going to read to the hon. member a passage from the Select Committee report. The hon. member has the report in front of him, and he will see that page 8, paragraph 3, reads as follows&#x2014;</p>

<block name="quote">In the ordinary way the board&#x2019;s private Bills are submitted for the consideration of, inter alia, the constituent authorities and the Union Department of Public Health. As this Bill deals exclusively with financial matters it was also submitted to the board&#x2019;s auditors, and by the Union Department of Public Health to the Treasury. All the constituent authorities, who take approximately 96 per cent. of the water supplied by the board, the auditors and the Treasury have expressed their approval of the board&#x2019;s proposals.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from><span class="col_2647-2648" refersTo="page_1333"/>Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>What about the Railway Administration?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#stratford">

<from>&#x2020;Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">STRATFORD</person>:</from>

<p>The Railway Administration has also expressed its approval. That, I think, is sufficient to satisfy the House that this is to all intents and purposes an agreed measure in so far as all the people&#x2014;or 96 per cent. of the people&#x2014;who might conceivably be interested have expressly been consulted and have expressly notified their approval. With regard to the other 4 per cent. they represent a very small body, but it is sufficient simply to note they have raised no objection to the Bill.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>One cannot have any objection to the principles contained in this regulation or against the amendment which has been brought about in the existing Act, especially after the investigation which was instituted by the Select Committee and the fact which the hon. member mentioned here, that the constituting body which is concerned has given its acquiescence. There are, however, other aspects which I would like to mention regarding this matter. In previous years we have repeatedly seen the regulations brought before the House in connection with the Rand Water Board. Bills are continually placed before the House and amendments are continually being made in the Acts relating to this Body. It has practically become a hardy annual that a private member introduces a Bill of which notice has been given in the newspaper, to amend the Acts relating to this body. Documents must be deposited and a fixed procedure must be followed, in regard to the time of the House which is occupied in order to bring about such an amendment. This Rand Water Board was brought into existence in the year 1904. It has existed now for 40 years, and we have noticed that amendments are continually being made in the Acts. I do not say that those amendments were not necessary.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_lands">

<from>*THE <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF LANDS</person>:</from>

<p>All the parties have always supported it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I do not say that it was unnecessary. I believe that we in this House have always found that the matter also has another side to it. The amendments were necessary. But this matter also has another side to it. The Rand Water Board has always extended its activities and practically every year it is demanded of this House that amendments must be made. On this occasion the amendments are apparently necessary. I was surprised to find that through all the years there were no such stipulations in the Acts. After the Rand Water Board has already been in existence for 40 years permission is now sought to build up a reserve fund; to make provision for depreciation and to provide for a renewal fund; for powers in connection with a uniform water tax, etc. Further authority is sought for the investment of certain balances. What have they done all these years? I cannot understand that such an important Body has been without these powers for all these years. In the nature of the thing every institution must have these powers. The point, however, is this, that we have a fixed procedure in this House in regard to private Acts. It is a long-drawn-out and expensive procedure. I do not want to criticise it because it is necessary for the protection of private interests. But it demands much time, and a great deal of trouble and expense is attached to it. This Body, which occupies an important position in the country, must, when it requires such an amendment, publish the amendment in the newspaper, send its officials to Cape Town, employ an advocate and attorney to appear before the Select Committee. Practically every year this. Body has these large expenses. It appears to me that in fact this need not be necessary. I do not mean that the procedure in this House should be less retarding. But why should such a Body be placed in the position that year after year it is put to that expense? I have no objection to this particular proposition. However, I do want to point out that individuals who object to the regulation and wish to object, are practically out of court. They cannot come to Parliament to put forward their objections because that entails travelling expenses; they must put in a petition and oppose the Bill. This is practically a court case and it means tremendous expenditure. I make these allegations because I contend that the Rand Water Board has practically become a public institution. It is no longer the small and negligible body it previously was. Let us go into its history. It was formed in 1904. At that time they were granted authority to take out 10,000,000 gallons of water per day. Later they were given the right to take 215,000,000 gallons of water per day from the Vaal River. Originally the area supplied was only the magisterial districts within the Witwatersrand. Later it was extended to the East Rand, and last year it was also extended to Pretoria and Vereeniging. I do not know precisely what the area supplied was in 1904, but in 1943 it was 1,959 square miles. Now, two years later, it is 3,980 square miles. The European population of that area supplied is approximately 645,900; the non-European population of that area is 938,920. The total is 1,584,820 It is quite apparent to us that the Rand Water Board does not supply a small number of people, but that it has become a tremendously large undertaking.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#stratford">

<from><span class="col_2649-2650" refersTo="page_1334"/>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">STRATFORD</person>:</from>

<p>What point do you wish to make in that connection?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>I am coming to that presently. I just want to point out what the circumstances are today. I have mentioned these figures and I asked whether it is still in the interests of the country that this Body should be a purely private Body in which the State has practically no say, except that the Statutes can be amended by Parliament only, that the Government appoints the Chairman and that the Minister of Transport appoints a member. The Vaal River is one of the main rivers in the country and we cannot allow this tremendously large undertaking at the Witwatersrand which is practically a private undertaking where it supplies such a tremendously large area, to take all that water from this river. This undertaking is no longer there merely to supply water to Johannesburg and the mines. It is extending rapidly. Originally the purpose was to supply water to Johannesburg, but subsequently this undertaking has been granted the right to extend its supply area and to supply water to industries far outside Johannesburg. It can supply water to industries outside Johannesburg. Since then institutions in Pretoria and Vereeniging have been included and water is now also supplied to industries at Vereeniging. According to the report of the Committee we read&#x2014;</p>

<block name="quote">Certain industries in and around Vereeniging have bought the right from the Board to take 29.2 million gallons of non-drinkable water per day.</block>

<p>In other words, this has now become a large business undertaking, similar to the Electricity Supply Commission. The Electricity Supply Commission supplies electricity to industries, municipalities, to the railways, etc. The Rand Water Board supplies water to the municipality of Johannesburg, to the mines, to Pretoria, to Vereeniging, to industries, just as the Electricity Supply Commission supplies electricity. It no longer uses the water for its own purposes only; it now sells it as well. I do no say that that is wrong, that it should not do so, but then I want to complain that we continue in this way that we leave it in the hands of the Rand Water Board as it is today. In my opinion the State ought to have much more say in the matter. Surely water is much more essential than electricity. Water is an essential service and we cannot allow it to continue in this way The Board consists of 34 members. Of them the Government appoints two, namely the chairman and another member, and the Railway Administration appoints one member. The Transvaal Chamber of Mines appoints 16. All the local authorities, from Johannesburg to Vereeniging&#x2014;all those mentioned here&#x2014;appoint 16 jointly. In other words, there are 32 members appointed over whom the State has no say. The State has merely two representatives, one of whom is the chairman, and he cannot always carry through his ideas. My attitude and contention is that the State should now step in, that where our valuable water is being used for certain places in the country, and is sold to industries for business undertakings, the State should step in just as it did in regard to electricity, and say: &#x201C;No, this thing has now assumed such proportions that we should have a body which exists as a Utility Commission, just like the Electricity Supply Commission, and that the State should have more representation on it. I am pleased that the Minister of Lands and Irrigation is listening to this matter. I think it is a matter which he should take into consideration and to which he should give his attention. He is a Minister who lately since he has become a Minister, has taken much interest in the water question, and who wants to build dams all over the country, and wants to conserve our country&#x2019;s water. But this tremendous dam is being used by a body over which the State has no say, in the manner I have described. I would say that if the position were to remain as it is today, then the Railways should have more say. If the Chamber of Mines has 16 representatives on the Board, I would like to see that the Railways have more than one.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#neate">

<from>Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">NEATE</person>:</from>

<p>Must I bring you a few volumes of Hansard?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>No, thank you, I can speak without the assistance of Hansard. I am not one of those persons who carries his brains in a book. I have my knowledge with me. (I do not carry my brains in Hansard; I have my brains in my head.)</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#conradie">

<from>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">J. H. CONRADIE</person>:</from>

<p>He does not understand it yet; talk Latin,</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>I contend that these points which I have raised do in fact demand the serious consideration of the Government. It will be interesting in connection with what has recently been said here in regard to the question of water supply at Hutchinson where a certain Minister receives 1s. 6d. per thousand gallons, to say that here the price is merely 11.2d. per thousand gallons. I am actually pleased, whereas the Minister charges 1s. 6d&#x201E; the Rand Water Board merely charges 11.2d. per thousand gallons. I think we should institute a Water Board at Hutchinson</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_lands">

<from>*THE <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF LANDS</person>:</from>

<p>Then you must also build a Vaal Dam there.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#swart">

<from><span class="col_2651-2652" refersTo="page_1335"/>*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">SWART</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; with the hori member for Beaufort West (Mr. Louw) as the Chairman. I have mentioned these points because I believe that, where in South Africa we have followed the principle for many years that public services, essential services, should not be left to individuals Where it can be helped, the State should have a say as we have done with our iron and steel and with electricity. That should be done in regard to water; and the Rand Water Board comes every year to Parliament with powers which are extended, and we should take this matter into serious consideration. I have no objection to these particular regulations which are being asked for today, because they are powers which any Board ought to have, and I can only express my surprise that they have not had them previously. I trust that the Minister will now give the whole matter his consideration in regard to the Whole question of water supply from the Vaal River, that the Government will intervene and see to it that it retains its say, and that everything goes well and does not develop into a tremendous undertaking which now supplies water to all kinds of industries. In these words I would like provisionally to support the Bill, but I hope that the matter will be put in order.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#le_roux">

<from>&#x2020;*Mr. <person refersTo="hansard_za">J. N. LE ROUX</person>:</from>

<p>I was one of the members who served on this Committee. The matter was before us last year and was put very plainly. There was a large committee which considered the matter, as well as an advocate who outlined the case very clearly, especially in regard to water supply in Pretoria. What was very upsetting to me, was the question of how it was going to effect settlers further down if we were going to pump out large volumes and supply industries with water. The purpose of this dam was principally to get settlers there and thus their interests come first. In the reply to the question they gave us the assurance that it did not detract from the rights of these people. Consequently we agreed that the distribution of water may be allowed. Recently the Rand Water Board again sent down a deputation. That was strange to me. I considered that the matter had been completed last year and that it would not come before us again. It appears to me that it is being made out that the Committee came down on a sort of a holiday.
